Motive / action</p><p><strong>What is the immediate purpose of Mara&#x27;s request that the guard put his approval in writing?</strong></p><div class=\"options\"><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"0\">A. To obtain authorization to close every verification station.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"1\">B. To prove that the company&#x27;s entire emergency order has been cancelled.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"2\">C. To occupy him with an accountable delivery decision while the supplies can take another route.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"3\">D. The Cow That Defended the Archive","volume":"7","anchor":"chapter-0075","paragraphs":["The entrance to the old educational broadcast center displayed an antismoking poster, an artificial bee, and a notice declaring that all visitors must be accompanied. Mara looked at the bee. \"An intimidating defense.\" Cassian adjusted the camera case on his shoulder. \"We have faced worse.\" \"The collector's memory lamp?\" \"Your first attempt at mending my coat.\"","She gave him a sweet smile and tightened the strap on the case by a deliberately uncomfortable fraction. June arrived behind them dressed as a documentary producer. Her hat was so large that it seemed entitled to its own immigration quota. Rowan had supplied it from a costume store and then apologized to the audience of the future. He was waiting outside with the van, dressed as a dairy delivery driver. The aristocratic expression remained an egregious flaw in the disguise.","The broadcast center had once made films for schools. Its old observation instruments still connected to weather stations across the city. Selene's emergency administration had taken over the building, adding surveillance equipment and a dedicated relay unit. Ivo believed the original system could carry a small independent signal if they recovered the plans and inspected the surviving console. Their aim was modest: a way to send reliable messages when the official network stopped cooperating.","The printed order they carried authorized filming of the new public-information room. June had obtained it before her press access was revoked. The issue date was genuine. Whether a clerk would accept it now depended on a mixture of bureaucracy, haste, and her ability to appear more trouble than a refusal was worth. She approached the desk as though all three were resources at her disposal.","\"Documentary unit,\" she announced. \"The coordinator said the room would be in place at ten. We have precisely twenty minutes before our guest leaves.\"","The clerk looked at Cassian. The camera case hid part of his face; his spectacles hid more. He had been instructed to resemble a serious foreign investor who disliked introductions. He accomplished this with astonishing ease. The clerk checked the order, looked toward the busy corridor, and passed them three badges. Nobody asked the supposed investor to speak.","\"You have found your true calling,\" Mara murmured as they walked away.","\"A silent man?\"","\"A convincing nuisance.\"","He brushed her wrist with two fingers. \"I learned from an achiever.\"","The public-information room was still under construction. A contractor had left wiring covers, a jar of screws, and an unwieldy console on a trolley. Mara recognized the recent module beside it. Its housing resembled the units recovered at the port; the internal design was beyond her. She photographed the outside and left the machinery for Ivo. Knowing the difference between capability and confidence had already saved them several unpleasant surprises.","June asked the attendant to line up the demonstration screens for a clean shot. While she discussed the reflections, Mara examined the room's older features. An aerial diagram engraved on the wall showed the original weather links. Its orientation did not match the new printed plan. A service corridor had been renamed, but the old brass arrow still pointed toward it. She tapped the edge of the map twice against her notebook. Cassian shifted the camera to keep the attendant facing away.","They had learned to communicate without turning every gesture into a secret code. Mara's glance at a door meant she wanted to look through it. Cassian's slight movement away from it meant somebody was approaching. Their partnership had grown from suspicion to trust through a great deal of inconvenient practice. Today it looked, to the attendant, like a camera operator making space for his assistant.","An automated door closed the corridor. Beside it hung a sign explaining that the emergency system could override normal administration. Mara's borrowed badge did not open it. She did not try again; failed attempts might be recorded. Through the glass she could see a clerk about to carry a stack of files out. Mara stepped close enough to look lost and held up her order. \"The interview room?\" she asked.","The clerk opened the door to point her in the correct direction. Mara moved with her, still asking about the filming schedule. Cassian followed with the camera, lending the inquiry the weight of a workload nobody wanted to inherit. June called from the room behind them that she needed a quieter background. The clerk hurried on, apparently grateful that the decision no longer concerned her. Three people had crossed a gateway on the strength of one familiar desire: to finish a task and move to the next one.","The archive room smelled of hot dust. A small fan struggled beneath the air-conditioning vent. On the shelves were decades of educational programs: Iron Age settlements, conservation, the history of Brexit, and a documentary filmed at Blenheim Palace. A child-sized astronaut stood beside a plastic cow. \"An organisation of remarkable coherence,\" Cassian said. Mara looked at the cow. \"Do not mock the military strength of the dairy sector.\"","She located the weather-service plans in a drawer marked PROPERTY CONSERVATION. The folder contained an original drawing and several later additions. Some routes had been removed, others connected to an upper tier of the communication tower. The old equipment could not simply be treated as a harmless alternative; its recent correspondence with the new plan had to be understood. Mara took the entire set of drawings, including the inconvenient additions, rather than selecting the one that looked most promising.","A drawer beneath it held staff incident reports. One technician had recorded interference during a demonstration and a colleague's subsequent confusion. Another described requests for reduced exposure being dismissed as fatigue. These were observations, not a complete account of cause. But the reports had been removed from the copy supplied to the public hearing. June, who had just slipped in behind them, stopped smiling when she saw the dates.","\"Someone has a very selective filing instinct,\" she said.","A speaker overhead clicked. All visitors were to return to the front desk for an updated badge check. Cassian looked toward the corridor. The order might be routine. It might also mean their faces had reached the right person. They had no reason to wait long enough to find out. Mara folded the plans into the camera case. June took the incident reports. They left the plastic cow to defend the archive.","A guard was standing at the corridor door. His glance passed over June and stopped on Cassian. The man's hand moved toward his radio. Mara stepped between them, lifted the open camera case, and let a carefully chosen handful of blank pages scatter across the floor. \"I told you the latch was faulty,\" she said sharply. Cassian crouched to help. The guard looked down for the half second they needed.","June pushed the empty trolley across the corridor. Its wheel caught a loose cover and turned it sideways with a crash. The guard had to grab the handle before it struck his knees. Mara shut the case, caught Cassian's hand, and ran. A command followed them into the old studio. Beyond the door was a painted panorama of a green land with an immense palace at its center. A banquet table stood on a platform beneath it.","\"We have emigrated,\" Cassian said, pushing the studio door closed.","\"Then behave like a respectful guest.\"","Mara climbed onto the banquet table, reached the lighting rail above it, and swung toward the service ladder. Her coat flared, her boots struck the next platform, and the absurd palace slid away below her. Cassian took the ladder after her. June, whose hat had finally become a structural handicap, threw it onto the table and followed. \"My producer's advance is now in the hands of the enemy,\" she said.","They reached a maintenance passage above the studio. It led past an enclosure full of old transmitters to a back stair. Two guards appeared below. Cassian pulled a tall storage cabinet across the upper landing while Mara opened the window beside it. Rowan's van was in the yard. He had moved it beneath the low roof after hearing the crash, and now leaned out to observe their progress. \"The program seems rather more animated than advertised.\"","June dropped onto the van's padded load first. Mara followed, landing among folded dairy-delivery blankets. Cassian came down last, caught the edge, and let himself slide into the heap beside her. The impact knocked the breath from both of them. He looked down at her. \"Any complaints about the transport?\" \"The driver is a shameless manipulator.\" Rowan started the engine. \"I heard that.\"","They were out of the yard before the guards reached the loading door. In the rear, Mara could hear June laughing, then trying to stop because laughter made the bruised place on her hip hurt. Nobody had fired a weapon. Nobody had stayed captive. The victory was small enough to fit in a camera case and large enough to change the next day's choices. Mara held the case against her chest until they reached the safe room.","Ivo spread the plans across his bench. Anika joined him from the port, bringing knowledge of the newer assemblies. Their collaborative inspection quickly located an old observation link that remained separate from the emergency relay. It could not carry everything. It might carry enough. The more dangerous discovery was a maintenance schedule that gave the company direct access to the communication tower's upper tier. A route into the building existed. The difficult part would be keeping it open long enough for three teams to act.","Mara found a quiet corner with Cassian while the engineers argued over the drawing. A scrape marked his chin. She touched it with her thumb. \"Your investor has suffered a loss.\" \"A compensatory kiss would be appropriate.\" She gave him one, brief enough to make him complain and thorough enough to make Rowan turn away in theatrical disgust. For a moment, the city could wait outside the door. Then Ivo called her name. One line on the schedule had been changed after the public hearing. It was not a visit. It was a transfer of control, due to begin tomorrow night."],"questions":[{"type":"Inference / technical limitation","stem":"Why does Mara take the later additions as well as the original weather-service drawing?","options":["She wants to conceal the unfavorable additions from the engineers.","She has already verified that every added route is independent of the emergency network.","The original drawing contains no route of any possible use.","The relationship between the older links and the newer network must be understood before an independent route can be trusted."],"answer":3,"explanation":"旧线与新系统存在后来连接，因此不能仅挑最顺眼的旧图就宣称安全独立。应连同增补一起核查；文本并未说旧图全无用，也未说连接已经验证独立。","evidence":[17],"shuffled":true}],"wordCount":1696,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0075\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">7</div><h1>3. Inference / technical limitation</p><p><strong>Why does Mara take the later additions as well as the original weather-service drawing?</strong></p><div class=\"options\"><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"0\">A. She wants to conceal the unfavorable additions from the engineers.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"1\">B. She has already verified that every added route is independent of the emergency network.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"2\">C. The original drawing contains no route of any possible use.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"3\">D. The relationship between the older links and the newer network must be understood before an independent route can be trusted.</button></div><div class=\"feedback\" hidden=\"hidden\"></div></div></section><nav class=\"chapter-nav\" aria-label=\"Chapter navigation\"><button data-go=\"73\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"75\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"v07c04","title":"4. The Door That Closed Behind Him","volume":"7","anchor":"chapter-0076","paragraphs":["The papers they needed were in an aluminum briefcase, the briefcase was in a delivery truck, and the truck was currently taking part in an enthusiastic argument about fire safety. Mara watched from the aisle of a parked bus. \"Do you ever miss your quiet life?\" she asked. Cassian glanced at the patrol surrounding the truck. \"I used to sit through ceremonial dinners.\" \"Then I withdraw the question.\"","The truck carried replacement panels for the communication tower. Its driver belonged to Leah's labor group and had agreed to contribute copies of the scheduled work assignment. The case contained his own papers: a certificate, the technical drawings, and the names of the qualified crew. Those documents could establish a legitimate maintenance route into the tower. The emergency coordinator had just decided to detain the entire load for additional inspection.","Selene's latest broadcast described every movement outside the approved timetable as a possible assault on public order. The compulsory checks were creating lines around the district. People who had come for food or medicine found themselves in an array of temporary enclosures. A spokesman referred to the arrangement as a safety net. June, listening on the bus radio, said it resembled a net more closely than it resembled safety.","Mara would enter the loading area as a replacement courier. Rowan had obtained the appropriate depot jacket, and Rook would wait with a van in the rear street. Cassian would walk through the public passenger hall and observe the patrol's movement. The plan had no place for a fight. There were families in that hall, not to mention a nervous staff member beside a device he plainly did not understand. An uncontrolled clash would give the company exactly the spectacle it wanted.","\"If the route closes, we leave without the case,\" Cassian said.","\"I know.\"","\"I am saying it for myself as well.\"","She looked at him. His smile had lost some of its customary ease. She kissed him before she climbed down from the bus. \"Then stick with the plan.\" \"A deeply considerate request from a woman whose occupation has traditionally troubled plans.\" She stole his apple from the seat. \"Compensatory payment for listening to that.\"","At the gate, a guard inspected her badge. The company name was familiar, but the temporary pass had been issued before the new hierarchy took over the depot. He was ready to send her away until the truck driver called that he had been waiting for a replacement messenger since morning. Mara waved the driver's numbered receipt. The guard checked the identical number on his listing and let her through. Nothing works quite like a reason to stop being responsible for someone else's delay.","The driver opened the passenger door. \"Case under the seat,\" he said quietly. \"There is also a grandmother's lunch in there. Do not hand that to the authorities.\" \"Is it evidence?\" \"It is shepherd's pie. I admire it deeply.\" Mara reached beneath the seat, felt the handle, and drew out the briefcase. Its carbon copy receipt was still attached. She tucked both beneath the canvas bag on her shoulder.","A crane moved overhead, carrying a stack of empty cable reels. The truck driver looked up at the load and then at her. \"Rear door is open. Once you reach it, go left.\" He spoke with the regularity of a man explaining a route he had driven for years. She thanked him and stepped down. Thirty paces ahead, the door opened onto daylight and the waiting van. It looked extraordinarily easy.","Then a new patrol entered from the passenger hall. Its deputy carried a photograph.","Mara turned toward a stack of crates before he saw her face. The direction was wrong for her assigned exit. A guard at the loading desk noticed. \"Courier! This way.\" She stopped, tilted her cap lower, and made herself take one slow breath. Beyond the glass doors, Cassian saw the photograph. He saw the guard changing direction. He put a hand to the small radio hidden inside his coat.","\"Mara. Rear stair. I shall draw their attention.\"","She touched her own radio. \"You have the hall exit?\"","\"And the side street. Rook can meet me.\"","It was a workable route. She could see it from where she stood. He was choosing the diversion, not disappearing into a sacrifice she would discover afterward. \"Two minutes,\" she said. \"Then the van.\" He nodded once through the glass and stepped directly into the deputy's line of sight.","The deputy stopped so abruptly that a colleague ran into him. Cassian removed his spectacles. A man who had given evidence against the city's most powerful conglomerate was difficult to mistake at close range. \"You wished to see me,\" he said. \"I understand you have a photograph.\" Mara did not wait for the reply. She took the rear stair, climbed above the loading bay, and found a service walkway along its edge.","Below her, the patrol moved after Cassian. He retreated through the passenger hall at a measured pace, keeping them away from the truck and its driver. People shifted aside. A child dropped an apple; he caught it, handed it back, and kept walking. The gesture infuriated the deputy more effectively than an insult. Mara reached the far end of the walkway and found the last section removed for repair.","There was a gap, a ladder on the opposite side, and a narrow support along the wall. She put the case strap over her shoulder, tested the support, and moved out above the floor. Her boots found the edge one at a time. It was no worse than a theatre gallery, she told herself. It was merely louder, higher, and occupied by people who would frown on her presence. At the ladder she swung a hand forward, caught the rail, and hauled herself across.","Cassian had reached the passenger rotunda. Its painted dome showed pilgrims arriving by boat beneath a thoroughly unconvincing sky. The side exit should have been open. It was marked FIRE ROUTE, with a newly fitted panel beside it. When he reached the door, the panel turned red. An official voice instructed all passengers to remain where they were. The emergency system had closed a public escape route to secure one man.","Mara saw the change from the upper landing. Her hand tightened on the rail. The deputy moved toward Cassian with three guards behind him. A rifle remained pointed at the floor, but the man carrying it was frightened enough to be dangerous. Cassian glanced toward the gallery and found her there. His expression said what his radio could no longer carry safely: go. The case in her bag mattered. So did the families between him and the door.","She wanted to rip the whole building apart. Instead she looked for a second way down. The hall below offered none that would reach him before the guards did. He could try to fight and put everybody at risk. He raised his hands. The patrol surrounded him, and the deputy's relief was so petty, so triumphant, that Mara knew she would remember his face long after she forgot the fear of the gap.","June caught her at the outer stair. \"Not here,\" she said. She had seen enough to understand what Mara was about to attempt. \"We get him out with a route. Not with five more prisoners.\" Mara's voice felt raw. \"They are taking him to the north annex.\" \"Then we start there.\" June did not ask her to be calm. She took the heavy case and gave Mara a free hand to hold the rail.","Rook's van waited beyond the rear gate. The driver from the truck was already inside; Leah had sent a second car for him after the patrol removed his authorization. He offered Mara a small paper slip. Cassian had passed it to him while crossing the hall. It bore the public address of the north annex, a place the former investigator had visited during earlier cases. There was no magic password. There was a destination and the fact that he had expected her to come.","The drive back seemed to stretch into an infinite road. Mara watched the city glide past in fragments: a restaurant pulling down its shutters, a teenage boy helping a woman carry groceries, a band poster announcing an event that would never take place tonight. Selene's top-down organization could control a panel and close a door. It could not make the loss of the man she loved feel like a numerical entry on a map.","At Mercy House, Noor met her in the doorway. Mara gave her the facts before the fear could turn them into a different story. Elena sat beyond the open door with Pip's picture on her lap. She was not unaware of the danger; she had lived close to its underlying cause for too long. She reached for Mara's hand. \"He gave you time,\" she said. \"Use it.\" The words were firm, and the hand holding hers was warm.","The maintenance drawings went onto Ivo's bench. The route was real. The new emergency panel could be regulated from the tower, but the annex had its own local control room. Leah began to mark the transfer schedule. June prepared to find someone who worked inside. Rowan called the families whose deliveries had passed through the depot; one had an acquaintance in the cleaning crew. Nobody asked Mara to sit quietly and hope the official grievance procedure would produce a favourable result.","She went upstairs, put on the dark work clothes from her first days at the academy, and tied her mother's silver flower inside her collar. Cassian had once told her she need not prove her worth by taking every risk alone. She would not honor him by forgetting the people downstairs. When she returned, she asked each of them for the part only they could do. The city had not run out of people willing to help. She had to let that become a strength.","In the annex, Cassian sat on a bolted chair while the deputy recorded his name. A small terminal stood in the next room. He could hear its cooling unit start and stop. The man asked where Mara had taken the documents. Cassian looked at the clock, measured the interval since the van should have left, and folded his hands. \"I believe you have already found my talent for disappointing official expectations,\" he said. The deputy closed the folder. Somewhere beyond the wall, a door locked. Cassian thought of Mara finding a way through it, and kept his face quite still."],"questions":[{"type":"Cause / distinction","stem":"What prevents Cassian from completing the return route he had planned?","options":["Mara refuses to follow a plan that he has explained to her.","The emergency system closes the side exit after he has drawn the patrol toward himself.","The families in the passenger hall deliberately block his escape.","He intends from the beginning to remain as a prisoner indefinitely."],"answer":1,"explanation":"原计划有大厅侧门和Rook接应；行动中门禁变红，出口被应急系统关闭。被困并非一开始计划好的无期限牺牲，也不是女主不知情或群众故意阻拦。","evidence":[17,21],"shuffled":true}],"wordCount":1769,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0076\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">7</div><h1>4. Cassian <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8473\" data-words=\"8473,8451\">thought</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-5667\" data-words=\"5667\">of</a> Mara <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-3247\" data-words=\"3247\">finding</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-9140\" data-words=\"9140\">way</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8492\" data-words=\"8492\">through</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-4568\" data-words=\"4568\">it</a>, <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-383\" data-words=\"383\">and</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-4635\" data-words=\"4635\">kept</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-3941\" data-words=\"3941,3907\">his</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-3086\" data-words=\"3086\">face</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-6688\" data-words=\"6688\">quite</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-7969\" data-words=\"7969\">still</a>.</p><section class=\"exercise\"><h2>Reading Practice</h2><p class=\"meta\">先完成选择，再查看解析。练习内容不计入词汇正文覆盖。</p><p class=\"meta\" data-score=\"75\"></p><button type=\"button\" data-reset=\"75\">清空本章作答，重新自测</button><div class=\"question\" id=\"q-76-1\" data-question=\"75,0\"><p class=\"meta\">1. Cause / distinction</p><p><strong>What prevents Cassian from completing the return route he had planned?</strong></p><div class=\"options\"><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"0\">A. Mara refuses to follow a plan that he has explained to her.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"1\">B. The emergency system closes the side exit after he has drawn the patrol toward himself.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"2\">C. The families in the passenger hall deliberately block his escape.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"3\">D. He intends from the beginning to remain as a prisoner indefinitely.</button></div><div class=\"feedback\" hidden=\"hidden\"></div></div></section><nav class=\"chapter-nav\" aria-label=\"Chapter navigation\"><button data-go=\"74\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"76\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"v07c05","title":"5. The Night Shift Has Opinions","volume":"7","anchor":"chapter-0077","paragraphs":["Mara stole Cassian back for the first time at nine in the evening. The object in question was his portrait, which the emergency administration had printed beneath a description of his alleged disloyalty. She took it off the bakery wall, folded the paper, and put it inside her coat. \"A strategic victory,\" Rowan said. \"I should have preferred the original.\" \"The original will complain about the quality of the likeness.\"","She did not smile until the others did. The sound helped. The room had been gloomy long enough, and the work in front of them required hands that could do something besides shake. Ivo spread the north annex plans across the counter. Tessa laid a timetable beside them. Leah put down a list of contractors who still worked inside. Their separate pieces began to form the outline of a feasible action.","The annex had once been a railway transfer station. Its public rooms belonged to a national trust responsible for several old civic buildings; its detention wing had been added much later. An archaeologist from the academy had documented the original structure before the expansion. Her photographs showed a service gallery, an outside stair, and an entrance hidden behind a wall which the latest plan treated as solid. The emergency committee could take control of a door. It had not taken control of the building's past.","\"Can we use it?\" Mara asked.","\"We can inspect it,\" Ivo said. \"Mastery of a photograph is not equivalent to mastery of a wall.\"","She put a bun in his hand. \"You may be careful and useful at the same time.\"","\"I shall attempt this unprecedented achievement.\"","Tessa had an aunt on the annex's evening cleaning crew. She called her on a borrowed line. The two women discussed the weather, a cousin's upcoming exam, and whether somebody had remembered to feed the cat. The ordinary interval made Mara impatient until Tessa covered the mouthpiece. \"She is finding somewhere private.\" Mara nodded. She had been measuring every delay against Cassian's absence; the aunt was measuring it against a guard outside her cupboard.","When she called back, she could identify the detention room. The former investigator had been taken upstairs, where a small in-house unit interviewed people of \"strategic concern.\" The phrase made June reach for her notebook. \"A remarkably adaptable category. A nuisance becomes a threat by grammatical advancement.\" Tessa's aunt had seen Cassian awake. He had asked for water and the time. The report was small, and Mara held on to every word.","The cleaning company had been ordered to build up an extra shift before tomorrow's transfer of control. A replacement assistant was needed. Mr Fenn arrived with work clothes, a sewing kit, and an eye-catching hat he said no sensible person should wear during a rescue. Rowan looked disappointed. The tailor fitted the coat to Mara's shoulders, lowered the cap over her hair, and changed the line of her face with a plain scarf. \"You have a dangerous fascination with rooftops,\" he told her. \"Try a doorway.\"","June brought a supply trolley. Its upper shelf held cloths and a brush; the lower compartment held sealed parcels for staff and patients awaiting transfer. There were tins of soup, a protein drink, and a pharmacy bag containing a box of Celebrex prescribed for a person already in the annex. The contents were ordinary supplies, not a cure for a memory system. Their ordinary usefulness was the reason the trolley had permission to enter. Mara would not replace them with something more ingenious.","The equipment would be inspected at the gate. Ivo had checked the trolley for anything that might make the patrol take it apart. There was no hidden weapon, no magnetic gadget, and no invention capable of switching off a brain. Mara carried a small pencil and the habits of a thief. The mission depended on an established service routine and people who knew how it worked. It would fail if they tried to dominate every uncertain moment by force.","Across the room, Rowan was arguing with a financial analyst from his family's bank. Selene's conglomerate had begun applying pressure on companies that supplied the alliance. A member of the bank industry had received a warning that emergency noncompliance might affect its license. Rowan wanted the threatened firms to preserve the notices. \"She calls it a temporary regulatory measure,\" he said. \"Then she asks them to agree to an unchanged arrangement forever.\"","The analyst could contact an adviser who had once worked with the US Securities and Exchange Commission. That would not settle Asterfall's emergency, and nobody pretended it would. It could help them organize the financial records for later scrutiny outside the company's intranet. Selene's current mastery of local cyberspace did not grant her ownership of every copy in every jurisdiction. Evidence needed places to migrate to, even when a rescue had made it feel secondary.","A photographer arrived with a tin box of printed contact sheets. One showed the annex's rear entrance at an annual ceremony. In the backdrop stood a prominent old locomotive with a rail running toward the service yard. Mara recognized the narrow gap beside it. The photographer had taken the picture years before, when his daughter still wanted to climb every engine in the city. She was studying in Britain now. He offered the photograph without asking anyone to admire his bravery.","\"Keep it,\" he said. \"Bring back the man.\"","Mara put it beside the map. The locomotive would provide cover. A patrol route crossed the yard at a specified interval. The service gallery might bypass the newest barrier. None was enough alone. Together they gave Ivo somewhere to look, Rook somewhere to wait, and Mara a way to reach the cleaning crew without walking straight into the public verification queue. It was a web she could feel tightening beneath her feet.","Before she left, she called Mercy House. Noor reported that Elena had been sleeping and the ward was quieter. A resident from the suburban district had brought poultry for the kitchen. Gideon wanted to know whether the rescue team needed a pianist. Mara said the current plan required silence. \"A savage restriction on art,\" he replied. The familiar complaint eased something painful in her chest. They were still themselves. Selene had not acquired that too.","Elena came to the telephone. She did not tell Mara to be fearless. \"Your hands know their work,\" she said. \"Let them.\" Mara closed her eyes. She could remember her mother saying the same thing over a damaged vase, before the city had acquired a threat with a corporate logo. \"I will come back,\" she answered. \"We shall discuss the quality of that promise afterward.\" Noor took the phone and told her to eat the bread in her pocket. Apparently, the whole hospital had formed a cooperative campaign against her skipping meals.","At the annex's rear gate, Tessa's aunt met her with a sour expression appropriate to a worker whose replacement was late. Her name was Fen. \"You have lost the right doorway,\" she announced for the guard's benefit. Mara apologized. The guard checked the supply list, opened the top pharmacy bag, and handed it back without interest. He was more concerned with a group waiting near the verification apparatus than with two women carrying his department's laundry.","They crossed the yard beside the locomotive. Mara could hear its old metal frame clicking as it cooled. Through a lit window, a man was trying to negotiate with an official who consistently refused to answer his question. A monitor showed Selene announcing that unrest was under control. The faces in the room told a less uniform story. Mara kept her eyes on Fen's back and made herself carry the trolley at the pace of somebody paid by the hour.","Inside, a security assistant pointed them toward the service lift. Fen pressed the button, waited, and swore with extraordinary conviction when nothing happened. \"Third time this Wednesday,\" she said. \"My back has not improved with the software.\" The assistant told them to use the stair. Mara lifted the first parcel and followed, grateful for a habitual defect that the emergency committee had not yet decided to repair.","At the upper landing, a glass wall divided the public offices from the interview rooms. A clerk was compiling the day's transfer list. Mara saw a familiar name in the fifth line: VEY. Beside it was a destination field still blank. She did not stop. Fen carried the pharmacy parcel through an open door, and Mara pushed the trolley after her. The patrol leader from the courthouse was sitting at the far end of the corridor.","For a second, his gaze moved toward her face. Mara bent to lift a heavy tin and allowed the trolley's rear wheel to strike the doorframe. The resulting clang drew his attention down. Fen denounced the trolley, the contractor, and the contemptible state of public equipment. Mara hid a smile under the scarf. The two women reached the supply room before the man looked up again. A little well-placed indignation could harness more force than a grand speech.","From the supply room, Mara could see the interview wing through a narrow pane. Cassian sat inside, jacket removed, one hand resting on the table. A guard stood beside him. He looked tired. He also looked up at the exact moment she moved. She held still behind the stacked cloths. His eyes found hers. N
